```python
    _print_report(
        flight, departure, scan_mean_rhi, scan_hours, source_label, metrics, result
    )
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

## scripts/screen_indian_routes.py

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Screen major Indian flight corridors for contrail formation and persistence.

Runs the Schmidt-Appleman criterion (SAC) along FL340 great-circle tracks
for four trunk routes under two atmospheric regimes:

A. Winter subtropical-jet regime (Jan-Feb).
B. Summer/monsoon regime (Jul-Aug).

Ambient profiles are prescribed as piecewise-linear (fraction, value)
knots per route. Output is a Markdown summary table on stdout.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import dataclasses
import sys
from pathlib import Path

PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
if str(PROJECT_ROOT) not in sys.path:
    sys.path.insert(0, str(PROJECT_ROOT))

from stratum.physics import thermo  # noqa: E402
from stratum.routes.corridor import (  # noqa: E402
    AIRPORTS,
    RouteProfileResult,
    evaluate_route_profile,
    great_circle_waypoints,
    piecewise_profile,
)

FL: float = 340.0
NUM_POINTS: int = 25
ETA: float = 0.30

CITIES: dict[str, str] = {
    "VIDP": "Delhi",
    "VABB": "Mumbai",
    "VOBL": "Bengaluru",
    "VECC": "Kolkata",
    "VAAH": "Ahmedabad",
}

WINTER: str = "Winter jet (Jan-Feb)"
SUMMER: str = "Summer/monsoon (Jul-Aug)"


@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)
class Scenario:
    """One route screened under one atmospheric regime."""

    origin: str
    destination: str
    season: str
    temp_knots: tuple[tuple[float, float], ...]
    rhi_knots: tuple[tuple[float, float], ...]


SCENARIOS: tuple[Scenario, ...] = (
    Scenario(
        "VIDP", "VABB", WINTER,
        temp_knots=((0.0, -52.0), (1.0, -44.0)),
        rhi_knots=((0.0, 0.45), (1.0, 1.15)),
    ),
    Scenario(
        "VIDP", "VECC", WINTER,
        temp_knots=((0.0, -54.0), (1.0, -47.0)),
        rhi_knots=((0.0, 0.65), (0.55, 1.00), (1.0, 1.25)),
    ),
    Scenario(
        "VABB", "VOBL", WINTER,
        temp_knots=((0.0, -38.0), (1.0, -34.0)),
        rhi_knots=((0.0, 0.30), (1.0, 0.75)),
    ),
    Scenario(
        "VIDP", "VAAH", WINTER,
        temp_knots=((0.0, -50.0), (1.0, -43.0)),
        rhi_knots=((0.0, 0.40), (1.0, 1.05)),
    ),
    Scenario(
        "VIDP", "VABB", SUMMER,
        temp_knots=((0.0, -35.0), (1.0, -38.0)),
        rhi_knots=((0.0, 0.55), (1.0, 0.95)),
    ),
    Scenario(
        "VIDP", "VECC", SUMMER,
        temp_knots=((0.0, -36.0), (1.0, -39.0)),
        rhi_knots=((0.0, 0.70), (1.0, 1.15)),
    ),
    Scenario(
        "VABB", "VOBL", SUMMER,
        temp_knots=((0.0, -35.0), (1.0, -37.0)),
        rhi_knots=((0.0, 0.45), (1.0, 0.85)),
    ),
    Scenario(
        "VIDP", "VAAH", SUMMER,
        temp_knots=((0.0, -35.0), (1.0, -38.0)),
        rhi_knots=((0.0, 0.40), (1.0, 0.80)),
    ),
)


def _explanation(result: RouteProfileResult) -> str:
    """Return a one-sentence physical explanation for a route result."""
    total = result["total_waypoints"]
    formation = result["formation_count"]
    persistence = result["persistence_count"]
    details = result["details"]
    # Persistent contrails require SAC and ice supersaturation at the same
    # waypoint; the two sets can be disjoint on a warming, moistening route.
    both = sum(1 for detail in details if detail["forms"] and detail["persists"])
    coldest_tlc = min(float(detail["TLC_C"]) for detail in details)

    if formation == 0 and persistence == 0:
        return (
            "No formation, no persistence: ambient T stays warmer than T_LC "
            f"(coldest threshold {coldest_tlc:.1f} C) and RHi stays <= 100%."
        )
    if formation == 0:
        return (
            "No formation: T > T_LC everywhere "
            f"(coldest threshold {coldest_tlc:.1f} C); ice-supersaturated air "
            f"at {persistence}/{total} waypoints is too warm for the SAC."
        )
    if persistence == 0:
        return (
            f"SAC holds on {formation}/{total} cold waypoints, but RHi stays "
            "<= 100% there: no ice supersaturation, contrails do not persist."
        )
    if both == formation == persistence:
        return (
            f"Persistent on {both}/{total} waypoints: T < T_LC coincides with "
            "ice-supersaturated (RHi > 100%) air."
        )
    if both == persistence:
        return (
            f"Persistent on {persistence}/{total} ice-supersaturated waypoints; "
            f"SAC additionally holds on {formation - both} cold, dry waypoints."
        )
    if both == formation:
        return (
            f"Persistent on all {formation} formation waypoints; a further "
            f"{persistence - both} ice-supersaturated waypoints stay above T_LC."
        )
    if both == 0:
        return (
            f"Formation ({formation}/{total}, cold-dry segment) and ice "
            f"supersaturation ({persistence}/{total}, warm-moist segment) do "
            "not overlap: no waypoint satisfies both, so no persistent "
            "contrail is supported."
        )
    return (
        f"SAC holds on {formation}/{total} and ice supersaturation on "
        f"{persistence}/{total} waypoints; {both} satisfy both, where "
        "persistent contrails occur."
    )


def main() -> int:
    """Execute the screening and print the Markdown summary table."""
    p_340 = thermo.fl_to_p(FL)
    print("Project STRATUM - Indian corridor contrail screening")
    print(
        f"FL{FL:.0f} ({p_340:.1f} hPa ISA), {NUM_POINTS} great-circle waypoints "
        f"per route, engine efficiency eta = {ETA:.2f}"
    )
    print()
    print("| Route | Season/Regime | Formation % | Persistence % | Physical Explanation |")
    print("| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |")

    for scenario in SCENARIOS:
        waypoints = great_circle_waypoints(
            AIRPORTS[scenario.origin], AIRPORTS[scenario.destination],
            num_points=NUM_POINTS, fl=FL,
        )
        temps = piecewise_profile(scenario.temp_knots, NUM_POINTS)
        rhi_values = piecewise_profile(scenario.rhi_knots, NUM_POINTS)
        result = evaluate_route_profile(waypoints, temps, rhi_values, eta=ETA)

        route = (
            f"{scenario.origin}-{scenario.destination} "
            f"({CITIES[scenario.origin]}-{CITIES[scenario.destination]})"
        )
        print(
            f"| {route} | {scenario.season} | {result['formation_pct']:.1f} | "
            f"{result['persistence_pct']:.1f} | {_explanation(result)} |"
        )
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
```

## scripts/verify_baseline.py

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Auditable baseline verification for Project STRATUM.

Recomputes the STRATUM verification quantities (mixing slope G, T_LM,
SAC points A/B/C, and ISA pressure conversion) and prints a comparison
table to stdout. Exit code is 0 when all checks pass, 1 otherwise.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import sys
from pathlib import Path

PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
if str(PROJECT_ROOT) not in sys.path:
    sys.path.insert(0, str(PROJECT_ROOT))

from stratum.physics import sac, thermo  # noqa: E402

P250_PA = 25000.0
ETA = 0.30

Row = tuple[str, str, str, str, bool]


def main() -> int:
    """Run the baseline checks and print the audit table."""
    rows: list[Row] = []

    def add_float(
        name: str,
        expected: float,
        actual: float,
        tolerance: float,
        fmt: str = "{:.10f}",
    ) -> None:
        ok = abs(actual - expected) <= tolerance
        rows.append(
            (name, fmt.format(expected), fmt.format(actual), f"{actual - expected:+.3e}", ok)
        )

    def add_bool(name: str, expected: bool, actual: bool) -> None:
        rows.append((name, str(expected), str(actual), "-", expected == actual))

    g = sac.G(P250_PA, ETA)
    add_float("G @ 250 hPa, eta=0.30 [Pa/K]", 1.6413706426, g, 1e-9)

    tlm_c = sac.solve_TLM(P250_PA, ETA) - 273.15
    add_float("T_LM @ 250 hPa, eta=0.30 [degC]", -41.9128742, tlm_c, 0.01, fmt="{:.7f}")

    add_float("ISA pressure at FL250 [hPa]", 376.015, thermo.fl_to_p(250.0), 0.05)
    add_float("ISA pressure at FL340 [hPa]", 249.996, thermo.fl_to_p(340.0), 0.05)

    t_k = 231.0
    h = 1e-5
    fd = (thermo.e_w(t_k + h) - thermo.e_w(t_k - h)) / (2.0 * h)
    rel_err = abs(thermo.d_e_w_dT(t_k) - fd) / fd
    add_float("d(e_w)/dT analytic rel. error @ 231 K [-]", 0.0, rel_err, 1e-8, fmt="{:.3e}")

    points = [
        ("A", -52.0, 0.60, True, False),
        ("B", -50.0, 1.12, True, True),
        ("C", -38.0, 0.58, False, False),
    ]
    context: list[tuple[str, float, float]] = []
    for name, t_c, rhi, forms, persists in points:
        result = sac.evaluate_sac(P250_PA, t_c + 273.15, rhi, eta=ETA)
        label = f"Point {name} (T={t_c:g} C, RHi={rhi:.0%})"
        add_bool(f"{label} forms", forms, result["forms"])
        add_bool(f"{label} persists", persists, result["persists"])
        context.append((label, float(result["TLC_C"]), float(result["TLM_C"])))

    check_w = max(len(r[0]) for r in rows)
    expected_w = max(len(r[1]) for r in rows)
    actual_w = max(len(r[2]) for r in rows)
    delta_w = max(len(r[3]) for r in rows)

    header = (
        f"{'CHECK':<{check_w}}  {'EXPECTED':>{expected_w}}  "
        f"{'ACTUAL':>{actual_w}}  {'DELTA':>{delta_w}}  STATUS"
    )
    print("STRATUM baseline verification")
    print(header)
    print("-" * len(header))
    for name, expected, actual, delta, ok in rows:
        print(
            f"{name:<{check_w}}  {expected:>{expected_w}}  "
            f"{actual:>{actual_w}}  {delta:>{delta_w}}  {'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'}"
        )

    print()
    print("Context (derived thresholds, 250 hPa, eta=0.30):")
    for label, tlc_c, tlm_c_ctx in context:
        print(f"  {label}: T_LC = {tlc_c:.3f} C (T_LM = {tlm_c_ctx:.3f} C)")

    failures = [r for r in rows if not r[4]]
    print()
    print(f"Checks passed: {len(rows) - len(failures)} / {len(rows)}")
    if failures:
        print("Baseline verification FAILED")
        return 1
    print("Baseline verification PASSED")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
```

## stratum/decide/climate.py

```python
"""Climate-benefit accounting for counterfactual altitude changes.

Contrail energy forcing (EF) is reported in Joules. To compare it against
the CO2 penalty of an extra-fuel maneuver, both sides are expressed as an
Earth-mean integrated radiative impact [J/m^2]:

- the avoided contrail term is ``EF / Earth surface area``;
- the CO2 term uses the Section 14.4 proxy that 1 kg CO2 carries an
  integrated radiative impact of ~3.5e-14 W/m^2 on a 20-year horizon,
  scaled linearly with the metric horizon (100-year reporting from the
  same proxy).

The linear horizon scaling is a documented proxy, not a full impulse
response model.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math

__all__ = [
    "CO2_INTEGRATED_FORCING_PER_KG_20YR",
    "EARTH_SURFACE_AREA_M2",
    "calculate_net_climate_benefit",
]

#: Integrated radiative impact proxy per kg CO2 on a 20-year horizon
#: [W/m^2 per kg] (Section 14.4).
CO2_INTEGRATED_FORCING_PER_KG_20YR: float = 3.5e-14

#: Earth surface area [m^2] used to convert contrail energy to a flux.
EARTH_SURFACE_AREA_M2: float = 5.10072e14

_BASE_HORIZON_YEARS: float = 20.0


def calculate_net_climate_benefit(
    avoided_contrail_ef_j: float,
    delta_co2_kg: float,
    metric_horizon_years: int = 20,
) -> dict[str, float | bool]:
    """Compare avoided contrail warming against the CO2 cost of a maneuver.

    Parameters
    ----------
    avoided_contrail_ef_j : float
        Contrail energy forcing avoided by the maneuver [J], >= 0.
    delta_co2_kg : float
        Extra CO2 emitted by the maneuver [kg], >= 0.
    metric_horizon_years : int, optional
        Reporting horizon for the CO2 impact, by default 20. The 20-year
        proxy from Section 14.4 is scaled linearly with the horizon
        (e.g. 5x at 100 years).

    Returns
    -------
    dict[str, float | bool]
        - ``avoided_ef_j``: echoed avoided energy forcing [J].
        - ``delta_co2_kg``: echoed CO2 penalty [kg].
        - ``net_benefit_positive``: True when the avoided contrail flux
          exceeds the CO2 flux over the metric horizon.
        - ``benefit_to_cost_ratio``: avoided contrail flux divided by the
          CO2 flux; ``inf`` when the CO2 cost is zero and the benefit is
          positive, ``0.0`` when both sides are zero.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        For negative energy or mass inputs, or a non-positive horizon.
    """
    if avoided_contrail_ef_j < 0.0:
        raise ValueError(
            f"avoided_contrail_ef_j must be non-negative, got {avoided_contrail_ef_j!r}"
        )
    if delta_co2_kg < 0.0:
        raise ValueError(f"delta_co2_kg must be non-negative, got {delta_co2_kg!r}")
    if metric_horizon_years <= 0:
        raise ValueError(
            f"metric_horizon_years must be positive, got {metric_horizon_years!r}"
        )

    horizon_scale = metric_horizon_years / _BASE_HORIZON_YEARS
    avoided_flux = avoided_contrail_ef_j / EARTH_SURFACE_AREA_M2
    co2_flux = delta_co2_kg * CO2_INTEGRATED_FORCING_PER_KG_20YR * horizon_scale

    if co2_flux > 0.0:
        ratio = avoided_flux / co2_flux
    elif avoided_flux > 0.0:
        ratio = math.inf
    else:
        ratio = 0.0

    return {
        "avoided_ef_j": float(avoided_contrail_ef_j),
        "delta_co2_kg": float(delta_co2_kg),
        "net_benefit_positive": bool(avoided_flux > co2_flux),
        "benefit_to_cost_ratio": float(ratio),
    }
```

## stratum/decide/counterfactual.py

```python
"""Counterfactual altitude evaluation for contrail-avoidance decisions.

For a flagged flight segment with a calibrated persistence probability,
this module evaluates the two primary counterfactual flight levels
(baseline +/- 2,000 ft), rejects physically infeasible changes, prices the
extra fuel with :mod:`stratum.decide.fuel`, prices the avoided contrail
energy forcing with :mod:`stratum.decide.climate`, and returns an
auditable decision record.

Decision policy:

- ``abstain``: probability below the 0.40 activation threshold; extra
  fuel is not justified.
- ``eligible``: at least one candidate has a positive net climate
  benefit; the best benefit-to-cost ratio is recommended.
- ``no_robust_candidate``: nothing passes the benefit test.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from typing import TypedDict

from stratum.decide.climate import calculate_net_climate_benefit
from stratum.decide.fuel import AIRCRAFT_CRUISE_BURN_KG_S, estimate_fuel_penalty

__all__ = [
    "ABSTAIN_THRESHOLD",
    "MAX_ALLOWED_FL",
    "MIN_ALLOWED_FL",
    "ALTITUDE_STEP_FL",
    "REFERENCE_SEGMENT_EF_J",
    "CandidateEvaluation",
    "AltitudeDecision",
    "evaluate_altitude_candidates",
]

#: Below this calibrated P(persist) the decision layer abstains.
ABSTAIN_THRESHOLD: float = 0.40

#: Standard ceiling and minimum cruise flight levels [hundreds of feet].
MAX_ALLOWED_FL: float = 390.0
MIN_ALLOWED_FL: float = 270.0

#: Primary alternative offset [hundreds of feet] (+/- 2,000 ft).
ALTITUDE_STEP_FL: float = 20.0

#: Reference contrail energy forcing of a flagged persistent segment [J].
#: Used to translate a persistence-probability reduction into an avoided
#: energy forcing; an audit constant, not a per-flight measurement.
REFERENCE_SEGMENT_EF_J: float = 1.0e10

#: Escape effectiveness of each direction (fraction of the flagged
#: segment's contrail energy forcing removed) when the layer is left.
_CLIMB_EFFECTIVENESS: float = 0.85
_DESCENT_EFFECTIVENESS: float = 0.80


class CandidateEvaluation(TypedDict):
    """Auditable evaluation record for one counterfactual flight level."""

    target_fl: float
    direction: str
    altitude_change_ft: float
    rejected: bool
    rejection_reason: str | None
    delta_fuel_kg: float
    delta_co2_kg: float
    penalty_pct: float
    avoided_ef_j: float
    benefit_to_cost_ratio: float
    net_benefit_positive: bool


class AltitudeDecision(TypedDict):
    """Decision record returned by :func:`evaluate_altitude_candidates`."""

    status: str
    baseline_fl: float
    baseline_p_persist: float
    recommended_fl: float | None
    delta_fuel_kg: float | None
    delta_co2_kg: float | None
    avoided_contrail_ef_j: float | None
    benefit_to_cost_ratio: float | None
    candidates: list[CandidateEvaluation]
    reason: str


def _evaluate_candidate(
    baseline_p_persist: float,
    baseline_fl: float,
    delta_fl: float,
    aircraft_type: str,
) -> CandidateEvaluation:
    """Evaluate one +/- ``ALTITUDE_STEP_FL`` candidate against all gates."""
    target_fl = baseline_fl + delta_fl
    change_ft = delta_fl * 100.0
    direction = "step climb" if delta_fl > 0.0 else "descent"

    rejection_reason: str | None = None
    if target_fl > MAX_ALLOWED_FL:
        rejection_reason = (
            f"target FL{target_fl:.0f} exceeds standard ceiling FL{MAX_ALLOWED_FL:.0f}"
        )
    elif target_fl < MIN_ALLOWED_FL:
        rejection_reason = (
            f"target FL{target_fl:.0f} below minimum cruise FL{MIN_ALLOWED_FL:.0f}"
        )
    if rejection_reason is not None:
        return CandidateEvaluation(
            target_fl=target_fl,
            direction=direction,
            altitude_change_ft=change_ft,
            rejected=True,
            rejection_reason=rejection_reason,
            delta_fuel_kg=0.0,
            delta_co2_kg=0.0,
            penalty_pct=0.0,
            avoided_ef_j=0.0,
            benefit_to_cost_ratio=0.0,
            net_benefit_positive=False,
        )

    penalty = estimate_fuel_penalty(aircraft_type, baseline_fl, target_fl)
    effectiveness = (
        _CLIMB_EFFECTIVENESS if delta_fl > 0.0 else _DESCENT_EFFECTIVENESS
    )
    avoided_ef_j = baseline_p_persist * effectiveness * REFERENCE_SEGMENT_EF_J
    benefit = calculate_net_climate_benefit(avoided_ef_j, penalty["delta_co2_kg"])
    return CandidateEvaluation(
        target_fl=target_fl,
        direction=direction,
        altitude_change_ft=change_ft,
        rejected=False,
        rejection_reason=None,
        delta_fuel_kg=penalty["delta_fuel_kg"],
        delta_co2_kg=penalty["delta_co2_kg"],
        penalty_pct=penalty["penalty_pct"],
        avoided_ef_j=avoided_ef_j,
        benefit_to_cost_ratio=float(benefit["benefit_to_cost_ratio"]),
        net_benefit_positive=bool(benefit["net_benefit_positive"]),
    )


def evaluate_altitude_candidates(
    baseline_p_persist: float,
    baseline_fl: float,
    aircraft_type: str = "A320",
) -> AltitudeDecision:
    """Evaluate the primary counterfactual flight levels for a segment.

    Parameters
    ----------
    baseline_p_persist : float
        Calibrated probability of contrail persistence for the flagged
        segment, in ``[0.0, 1.0]``.
    baseline_fl : float
        Current flight level, in hundreds of feet (FL350 -> ``350.0``).
    aircraft_type : str, optional
        Aircraft type for the fuel penalty model, by default ``"A320"``.

    Returns
    -------
    AltitudeDecision
        Decision record with ``status`` one of ``"abstain"``,
        ``"eligible"``, ``"no_robust_candidate"``, the baseline state,
        the recommendation (when eligible), and the per-candidate
        evaluation records.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        For a probability outside ``[0.0, 1.0]``, a non-positive baseline
        flight level, or an unsupported aircraft type.
    """
    p_persist = float(baseline_p_persist)
    if not math.isfinite(p_persist) or not 0.0 <= p_persist <= 1.0:
        raise ValueError(
            f"baseline_p_persist must be in [0.0, 1.0], got {baseline_p_persist!r}"
        )
    if baseline_fl <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError(f"baseline_fl must be positive, got {baseline_fl!r}")
    key = aircraft_type.strip().upper()
    if key not in AIRCRAFT_CRUISE_BURN_KG_S:
        supported = ", ".join(sorted(AIRCRAFT_CRUISE_BURN_KG_S))
        raise ValueError(
            f"unsupported aircraft_type {aircraft_type!r}; expected one of {supported}"
        )

    if p_persist < ABSTAIN_THRESHOLD:
        return AltitudeDecision(
            status="abstain",
            baseline_fl=baseline_fl,
            baseline_p_persist=p_persist,
            recommended_fl=None,
            delta_fuel_kg=None,
            delta_co2_kg=None,
            avoided_contrail_ef_j=None,
            benefit_to_cost_ratio=None,
            candidates=[],
            reason=(
                f"P(persist)={p_persist:.2f} is below the "
                f"{ABSTAIN_THRESHOLD:.2f} activation threshold; extra fuel "
                "is not justified."
            ),
        )

    candidates = [
        _evaluate_candidate(p_persist, baseline_fl, +ALTITUDE_STEP_FL, aircraft_type),
        _evaluate_candidate(p_persist, baseline_fl, -ALTITUDE_STEP_FL, aircraft_type),
    ]
    eligible = [
        candidate
        for candidate in candidates
        if not candidate["rejected"] and candidate["net_benefit_positive"]
    ]
    if not eligible:
        return AltitudeDecision(
            status="no_robust_candidate",
            baseline_fl=baseline_fl,
            baseline_p_persist=p_persist,
            recommended_fl=None,
            delta_fuel_kg=None,
            delta_co2_kg=None,
            avoided_contrail_ef_j=None,
            benefit_to_cost_ratio=None,
            candidates=candidates,
            reason="No counterfactual flight level passes the net-benefit test.",
        )

    best = max(eligible, key=lambda candidate: candidate["benefit_to_cost_ratio"])
    return AltitudeDecision(
        status="eligible",
        baseline_fl=baseline_fl,
        baseline_p_persist=p_persist,
        recommended_fl=best["target_fl"],
        delta_fuel_kg=best["delta_fuel_kg"],
        delta_co2_kg=best["delta_co2_kg"],
        avoided_contrail_ef_j=best["avoided_ef_j"],
        benefit_to_cost_ratio=best["benefit_to_cost_ratio"],
        candidates=candidates,
        reason=(
            f"FL{best['target_fl']:.0f} ({best['direction']}) passes the "
            "net-benefit test with the best benefit-to-cost ratio."
        ),
    )
```

## stratum/decide/fuel.py

```python
"""Fuel penalty estimates for counterfactual altitude changes.

Section 14.4 of the STRATUM paper: a 2,000 ft step climb of a typical
narrow-body transport (A320/B737, nominal cruise burn ~0.65 kg/s) costs
roughly +1.5-2.5% of the fuel burned during the maneuver window, while a
2,000 ft descent costs +0.8-1.5% because of the higher air density at the
lower level. Both alternatives burn *extra* fuel; the decision layer must
weigh that cost against the avoided contrail energy forcing.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = [
    "CO2_PER_KG_FUEL",
    "AIRCRAFT_CRUISE_BURN_KG_S",
    "estimate_fuel_penalty",
]

#: CO2 released per kilogram of burned jet fuel [kg(CO2)/kg(fuel)]
#: (Section 14.4).
CO2_PER_KG_FUEL: float = 3.16

#: Nominal cruise fuel burn [kg/s] per supported aircraft type.
AIRCRAFT_CRUISE_BURN_KG_S: dict[str, float] = {
    "A320": 0.65,
    "B737": 0.65,
}

#: Midpoints [%] of the Section 14.4 penalty ranges, per 2,000 ft change.
_CLIMB_PENALTY_PCT_PER_2000FT: float = 2.0
_DESCENT_PENALTY_PCT_PER_2000FT: float = 1.15

_FT_PER_FL: float = 100.0
_REFERENCE_STEP_FT: float = 2000.0
_SECONDS_PER_MINUTE: float = 60.0


def estimate_fuel_penalty(
    aircraft_type: str,
    baseline_fl: float,
    target_fl: float,
    duration_min: float = 20.0,
) -> dict[str, float]:
    """Estimate the extra fuel burned by a 2,000 ft altitude change.

    Flight levels are given in hundreds of feet (FL350 -> ``350.0``).
    The penalty applies to the fuel burned during ``duration_min`` at the
    nominal cruise burn of ``aircraft_type``. Both a step climb and a
    descent burn extra fuel relative to level cruise at the baseline
    flight level.

    Parameters
    ----------
    aircraft_type : str
        Supported types: ``"A320"``, ``"B737"`` (case-insensitive).
    baseline_fl : float
        Current flight level, in hundreds of feet.
    target_fl : float
        Counterfactual flight level, in hundreds of feet.
    duration_min : float, optional
        Analysis window after the level change [min], by default 20.

    Returns
    -------
    dict[str, float]
        - ``delta_fuel_kg``: extra fuel [kg], always >= 0.
        - ``delta_co2_kg``: ``delta_fuel_kg * CO2_PER_KG_FUEL`` [kg].
        - ``penalty_pct``: penalty relative to window fuel [%].

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        For an unsupported aircraft type or non-positive inputs.
    """
    key = aircraft_type.strip().upper()
    if key not in AIRCRAFT_CRUISE_BURN_KG_S:
        supported = ", ".join(sorted(AIRCRAFT_CRUISE_BURN_KG_S))
        raise ValueError(
            f"unsupported aircraft_type {aircraft_type!r}; expected one of {supported}"
        )
    if baseline_fl <= 0.0 or target_fl <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError(
            f"flight levels must be positive, got {baseline_fl!r} and {target_fl!r}"
        )
    if duration_min <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError(f"duration_min must be positive, got {duration_min!r}")

    change_ft = (target_fl - baseline_fl) * _FT_PER_FL
    if change_ft == 0.0:
        return {"delta_fuel_kg": 0.0, "delta_co2_kg": 0.0, "penalty_pct": 0.0}

    window_fuel_kg = (
        AIRCRAFT_CRUISE_BURN_KG_S[key] * duration_min * _SECONDS_PER_MINUTE
    )
    magnitude = abs(change_ft) / _REFERENCE_STEP_FT
    if change_ft > 0.0:
        base_pct = _CLIMB_PENALTY_PCT_PER_2000FT
    else:
        base_pct = _DESCENT_PENALTY_PCT_PER_2000FT
    penalty_pct = base_pct * magnitude
    delta_fuel_kg = window_fuel_kg * penalty_pct / 100.0
    return {
        "delta_fuel_kg": delta_fuel_kg,
        "delta_co2_kg": delta_fuel_kg * CO2_PER_KG_FUEL,
        "penalty_pct": penalty_pct,
    }
```

## stratum/detect/btd.py

```python
"""Brightness-temperature-difference (BTD) computation and normalization.

The classical satellite contrail detector uses the split-window
brightness temperature difference BTD = BT10.8 - BT12.0, which is
slightly positive for thin ice clouds (contrails, cirrus) and near zero
for clear sky. A local median background is removed and the residual is
scaled by a local robust standard deviation so that matched-filter
thresholds are expressed in units of local noise.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage

__all__ = ["compute_btd", "normalize_btd"]

#: Gaussian-consistency factor converting MAD to an equivalent sigma.
MAD_TO_SIGMA: float = 1.4826
#: Floor for the local robust sigma, preventing division by zero.
MIN_ROBUST_SIGMA: float = 1e-3


def _validate_2d(array: np.ndarray, name: str) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return ``array`` as a float ndarray, enforcing 2D shape."""
    result = np.asarray(array, dtype=float)
    if result.ndim != 2:
        raise ValueError(f"{name} must be a 2D array, got ndim={result.ndim}")
    return result


def compute_btd(bt108: np.ndarray, bt120: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Compute the split-window brightness temperature difference.

    Parameters
    ----------
    bt108 : np.ndarray
        Brightness temperature at 10.8 um [K].
    bt120 : np.ndarray
        Brightness temperature at 12.0 um [K], same shape as ``bt108``.

    Returns
    -------
    np.ndarray
        ``BTD = bt108 - bt120`` [K].
    """
    bt108 = _validate_2d(bt108, "bt108")
    bt120 = _validate_2d(bt120, "bt120")
    if bt108.shape != bt120.shape:
        raise ValueError(
            f"bt108 and bt120 must have the same shape, got {bt108.shape} and {bt120.shape}"
        )
    return bt108 - bt120


def normalize_btd(btd: np.ndarray, kernel_size: int = 15) -> np.ndarray:
    """Normalize BTD by a local median background and local robust sigma.

    The background is a ``kernel_size x kernel_size`` median filter; the
    residual (``btd - background``) is divided by ``1.4826 * MAD`` of the
    local residual, so the output is in units of local robust standard
    deviations. The sigma is clamped to ``MIN_ROBUST_SIGMA`` to prevent
    division by zero on constant fields.

    Parameters
    ----------
    btd : np.ndarray
        Brightness temperature difference [K].
    kernel_size : int, optional
        Window length in pixels for both the background and the local
        MAD filters, by default 15.

    Returns
    -------
    np.ndarray
        Standardized BTD field [robust sigma units], same shape as ``btd``.
    """
    if kernel_size < 1:
        raise ValueError(f"kernel_size must be >= 1, got {kernel_size}")
    btd = _validate_2d(btd, "btd")

    background = ndimage.median_filter(btd, size=kernel_size, mode="reflect")
    residual = btd - background
    local_mad = ndimage.median_filter(np.abs(residual), size=kernel_size, mode="reflect")
    sigma = np.maximum(MAD_TO_SIGMA * local_mad, MIN_ROBUST_SIGMA)
    return residual / sigma
```

## stratum/detect/filters.py

```python
"""Oriented matched filters and line extraction for contrail detection.

Builds elongated zero-mean line kernels at discrete orientations,
convolves the normalized BTD field with each kernel, keeps the maximum
response per pixel, applies hysteresis thresholding, and extracts
connected line objects that pass length and aspect-ratio gates.

Angles are measured counter-clockwise from the column (x) axis of the
array, with rows representing the y axis. All reported angles lie in
[0, 180).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from typing import TypedDict

import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage

from stratum.detect.btd import compute_btd, normalize_btd

__all__ = [
    "DetectedObject",
    "DetectionResult",
    "build_oriented_line_kernels",
    "detect_contrail_lines",
]

#: Hysteresis weak-threshold as a fraction of the primary threshold.
HYSTERESIS_WEAK_FRACTION: float = 0.7
#: Minimum object aspect ratio (length / width) accepted as a line.
MIN_ASPECT_RATIO: float = 3.0
#: Negative flank width of each kernel, in pixels.
FLANK_WIDTH_PX: int = 1
#: 8-connectivity structure for connected-component labelling.
_CONNECTIVITY: np.ndarray = np.ones((3, 3), dtype=bool)


class DetectedObject(TypedDict):
    """A detected contrail-like line object.

    Attributes
    ----------
    centroid : (float, float)
        Mean (row, col) position of the object mask [pixels].
    length_px : float
        Extent along the principal axis [pixels].
    angle_deg : float
        Principal-axis orientation in [0, 180) [deg].
    mean_btd : float
        Mean raw BTD of the object mask [K].
    """

    centroid: tuple[float, float]
    length_px: float
    angle_deg: float
    mean_btd: float


class DetectionResult(TypedDict):
    """Result of :func:`detect_contrail_lines`.

    Attributes
    ----------
    num_detected : int
        Number of accepted line objects.
    objects : list of DetectedObject
        Accepted line objects.
    max_response_map : np.ndarray
        Per-pixel maximum matched-filter response [robust sigma units].
    """

    num_detected: int
    objects: list[DetectedObject]
    max_response_map: np.ndarray


def build_oriented_line_kernels(
    length: int = 11, width: int = 3, num_angles: int = 8
) -> list[np.ndarray]:
    """Build elongated zero-mean matched-filter kernels.

    Each kernel has a positive line core of the requested ``width`` and
    negative flanks (``FLANK_WIDTH_PX`` pixels each side) along a line of
    the requested ``length``. Core and flank weights are normalized by
    their pixel counts so that every kernel sums to zero, making the
    response vanish on any uniform background.

    Parameters
    ----------
    length : int, optional
        Line length in pixels, by default 11. Rounded up to an odd
        support size so a pixel centre exists.
    width : int, optional
        Positive core width in pixels, by default 3.
    num_angles : int, optional
        Number of orientations spanning [0, 180), by default 8.

    Returns
    -------
    list of np.ndarray
        ``num_angles`` kernels of shape ``(support, support)``.
    """
    if length < 3:
        raise ValueError(f"length must be >= 3, got {length}")
    if width < 1:
        raise ValueError(f"width must be >= 1, got {width}")
    if num_angles < 1:
        raise ValueError(f"num_angles must be >= 1, got {num_angles}")

    support = length if length % 2 == 1 else length + 1
    half_length = length / 2.0
    half_width = width / 2.0
    grid_y, grid_x = np.mgrid[0:support, 0:support] - support // 2

    kernels: list[np.ndarray] = []
    for index in range(num_angles):
        theta = math.pi * index / num_angles
        cos_t, sin_t = math.cos(theta), math.sin(theta)
        along = grid_x * cos_t + grid_y * sin_t
        perpendicular = -grid_x * sin_t + grid_y * cos_t

        core = (np.abs(perpendicular) <= half_width) & (np.abs(along) <= half_length)
        flank = (
            (np.abs(perpendicular) > half_width)
            & (np.abs(perpendicular) <= half_width + FLANK_WIDTH_PX)
            & (np.abs(along) <= half_length)
        )
        if not core.any() or not flank.any():
            raise ValueError(
                f"kernel geometry is degenerate for index {index} "
                f"(length={length}, width={width})"
            )

        kernel = np.zeros((support, support), dtype=float)
        kernel[core] = 1.0 / float(core.sum())
        kernel[flank] -= 1.0 / float(flank.sum())
        kernels.append(kernel)
    return kernels


def _object_from_mask(
    mask: np.ndarray, btd: np.ndarray, min_length_px: int
) -> DetectedObject | None:
    """Build a DetectedObject from a component mask, applying shape gates.

    Returns ``None`` when the component fails the minimum length or
    aspect-ratio gate.
    """
    coords = np.argwhere(mask)
    if coords.shape[0] < 2:
        return None

    centre = coords.mean(axis=0)
    centred = coords - centre
    covariance = centred.T @ centred / float(len(centred))
    eigenvalues, eigenvectors = np.linalg.eigh(covariance)
    principal_axis = eigenvectors[:, -1]
    minor_axis = eigenvectors[:, 0]

    along = centred @ principal_axis
    length_px = float(along.max() - along.min() + 1.0)
    # 4 * sigma of the minor axis approximates the object width for a
    # uniform bar (sqrt(12) / 4 = 1.155 * width) and is robust to lone
    # outlier pixels inflating a raw extent.
    width_px = max(4.0 * math.sqrt(max(float(eigenvalues[0]), 0.0)), 1.0)

    if length_px < min_length_px or length_px / width_px < MIN_ASPECT_RATIO:
        return None

    angle_deg = math.degrees(math.atan2(principal_axis[0], principal_axis[1])) % 180.0
    return {
        "centroid": (float(centre[0]), float(centre[1])),
        "length_px": length_px,
        "angle_deg": angle_deg,
        "mean_btd": float(btd[mask].mean()),
    }


def detect_contrail_lines(
    bt108: np.ndarray, bt120: np.ndarray, threshold: float = 2.0, min_length_px: int = 8
) -> DetectionResult:
    """Detect contrail-like lines in a split-window scene.

    Pipeline: compute and normalize BTD, convolve with the eight oriented
    line kernels while retaining the maximum response and best angle per
    pixel, apply hysteresis thresholding (weak = ``0.7 * threshold``),
    then label connected components and keep objects whose principal
    length is at least ``min_length_px`` and whose aspect ratio is at
    least ``MIN_ASPECT_RATIO``.

    Parameters
    ----------
    bt108 : np.ndarray
        Brightness temperature at 10.8 um [K].
    bt120 : np.ndarray
        Brightness temperature at 12.0 um [K], same shape as ``bt108``.
    threshold : float, optional
        Primary response threshold [robust sigma units], by default 2.0.
    min_length_px : int, optional
        Minimum accepted object length in pixels, by default 8.

    Returns
    -------
    DetectionResult
        ``num_detected``, per-object descriptors and the maximum-response
        map.
    """
    if threshold <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError(f"threshold must be positive, got {threshold}")
    if min_length_px < 1:
        raise ValueError(f"min_length_px must be >= 1, got {min_length_px}")

    btd = compute_btd(bt108, bt120)
    normalized = normalize_btd(btd)

    response = np.full(btd.shape, -np.inf, dtype=float)
    angle_index = np.zeros(btd.shape, dtype=int)
    for index, kernel in enumerate(build_oriented_line_kernels()):
        kernel_response = ndimage.convolve(normalized, kernel, mode="reflect")
        better = kernel_response > response
        response = np.where(better, kernel_response, response)
        angle_index = np.where(better, index, angle_index)

    strong = response >= threshold
    weak = response >= HYSTERESIS_WEAK_FRACTION * threshold
    labels, num_labels = ndimage.label(weak, structure=_CONNECTIVITY)

    objects: list[DetectedObject] = []
    if num_labels:
        strong_labels = np.unique(labels[strong])
        for label in strong_labels:
            if label == 0:
                continue
            obj = _object_from_mask(labels == label, btd, min_length_px)
            if obj is not None:
                objects.append(obj)

    return {
        "num_detected": len(objects),
        "objects": objects,
        "max_response_map": response,
    }
```

## stratum/io/adsb_schema.py

```python
"""Canonical ADS-B trajectory schema and cleaning for Project STRATUM.

Section 6.4 defines the canonical trajectory-point schema ingested from
OpenSky (`source='opensky'`) and ADS-B Exchange (`source='adsbx'`):

- ``icao24`` (str, lowercase), ``time`` (UTC), ``lat``/``lon`` (deg),
- ``baro_alt_ft`` and nullable ``geo_alt_ft`` [ft],
- ``gs_kt`` [kt], ``track_deg`` [deg], ``vrate_fpm`` [ft/min],
- ``callsign`` (str, stripped), ``on_ground`` (bool), ``source`` (str).

:func:`clean_trajectory_points` applies the ingestion filters: airborne
rows only, cruise retention (baro altitude >= FL250), the Indian domain
bounding box, kinematic plausibility, and de-duplication.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

__all__ = [
    "ADSB_COLUMNS",
    "REQUIRED_COLUMNS",
    "CRUISE_MIN_BARO_ALT_FT",
    "LATITUDE_BOUNDS_DEG",
    "LONGITUDE_BOUNDS_DEG",
    "MAX_ABS_VRATE_FPM",
    "MAX_GROUND_SPEED_JUMP_KT",
    "clean_trajectory_points",
]

#: Canonical column order of the cleaned trajectory frame.
ADSB_COLUMNS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "icao24",
    "time",
    "lat",
    "lon",
    "baro_alt_ft",
    "geo_alt_ft",
    "gs_kt",
    "track_deg",
    "vrate_fpm",
    "callsign",
    "on_ground",
    "source",
)

#: Columns that must be present in the raw input; ``geo_alt_ft`` is the
#: only nullable column and is created when absent.
REQUIRED_COLUMNS: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(
    name for name in ADSB_COLUMNS if name != "geo_alt_ft"
)

#: Cruise retention floor [ft]: keep FL250 and above.
CRUISE_MIN_BARO_ALT_FT: float = 25000.0

#: Indian domain bounding box for latitude and longitude [deg].
LATITUDE_BOUNDS_DEG: tuple[float, float] = (6.0, 37.0)
LONGITUDE_BOUNDS_DEG: tuple[float, float] = (68.0, 98.0)

#: Kinematic plausibility gates.
MAX_ABS_VRATE_FPM: float = 6000.0
MAX_GROUND_SPEED_JUMP_KT: float = 100.0

_NUMERIC_COLUMNS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "lat",
    "lon",
    "baro_alt_ft",
    "geo_alt_ft",
    "gs_kt",
    "track_deg",
    "vrate_fpm",
)


def clean_trajectory_points(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Clean raw ADS-B points into the canonical schema (Section 6.4).

    Filters applied in order:

    1. type normalization (UTC time, lowercase ``icao24``, stripped
       ``callsign``, numeric columns coerced, lowercased ``source``);
    2. drop ``on_ground`` rows and rows missing ``time``/``lat``/``lon``/
       ``baro_alt_ft``;
    3. cruise retention: ``baro_alt_ft >= CRUISE_MIN_BARO_ALT_FT``;
    4. Indian domain bounding box;
    5. sort by ``(icao24, time)`` and drop duplicate fixes;
    6. kinematic plausibility: drop a point when its vertical rate
       exceeds ``MAX_ABS_VRATE_FPM`` or its ground speed jumps by more
       than ``MAX_GROUND_SPEED_JUMP_KT`` relative to the previous fix of
       the same aircraft.

    Parameters
    ----------
    df : pandas.DataFrame
        Raw points containing at least :data:`REQUIRED_COLUMNS`.

    Returns
    -------
    pandas.DataFrame
        Cleaned frame with exactly :data:`ADSB_COLUMNS`, sorted by
        ``(icao24, time)`` and re-indexed from 0.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If a required column is missing.
    """
    missing = [name for name in REQUIRED_COLUMNS if name not in df.columns]
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(f"ADS-B frame is missing required columns: {missing}")

    cleaned = df.copy()
    if "geo_alt_ft" not in cleaned.columns:
        cleaned["geo_alt_ft"] = np.nan

    cleaned["time"] = pd.to_datetime(cleaned["time"], utc=True)
    for name in _NUMERIC_COLUMNS:
        cleaned[name] = pd.to_numeric(cleaned[name], errors="coerce")
    cleaned["icao24"] = cleaned["icao24"].astype(str).str.strip().str.lower()
    cleaned["callsign"] = cleaned["callsign"].astype(str).str.strip()
    cleaned["source"] = cleaned["source"].astype(str).str.strip().str.lower()

    cleaned = cleaned.dropna(subset=["time", "lat", "lon", "baro_alt_ft"])
    on_ground = cleaned["on_ground"].fillna(False).astype(bool)
    cleaned = cleaned[~on_ground]
    cleaned = cleaned[cleaned["baro_alt_ft"] >= CRUISE_MIN_BARO_ALT_FT]
    cleaned = cleaned[
        cleaned["lat"].between(*LATITUDE_BOUNDS_DEG)
        & cleaned["lon"].between(*LONGITUDE_BOUNDS_DEG)
    ]

    cleaned = cleaned.sort_values(["icao24", "time"], kind="mergesort")
    cleaned = cleaned.drop_duplicates(subset=["icao24", "time"], keep="first")

    previous_gs = cleaned.groupby("icao24", sort=False)["gs_kt"].shift(1)
    ground_speed_jump = (cleaned["gs_kt"] - previous_gs).abs()
    plausible = (cleaned["vrate_fpm"].abs() <= MAX_ABS_VRATE_FPM) & ~(
        ground_speed_jump > MAX_GROUND_SPEED_JUMP_KT
    )
    cleaned = cleaned[plausible]

    return cleaned.loc[:, list(ADSB_COLUMNS)].reset_index(drop=True)
```

## stratum/io/era5.py

```python
"""ERA5 reanalysis ingestion and point interpolation for STRATUM.

The ingestion layer targets the Indian subdomain used by the pipeline:

- bounding box ``(North, West, South, East) = (32.0, 68.0, 15.0, 82.0)``,
- pressure levels ``[200, 225, 250, 300, 350, 400]`` hPa,
- the six meteorological variables required by the correction model.

:func:`load_era5_dataset` validates a NetCDF file, and
:func:`interpolate_met_to_segment` extracts local ambient values at an
arbitrary ``(lat, lon, pressure, time)`` point through multi-dimensional
linear interpolation.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from typing import Any

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import xarray as xr

__all__ = [
    "ERA5_BBOX",
    "ERA5_PRESSURE_LEVELS_HPA",
    "ERA5_VARIABLES",
    "load_era5_dataset",
    "interpolate_met_to_segment",
]

#: Domain bounding box (North, West, South, East) [deg].
ERA5_BBOX: tuple[float, float, float, float] = (32.0, 68.0, 15.0, 82.0)

#: Pressure levels [hPa] stored for the pipeline.
ERA5_PRESSURE_LEVELS_HPA: tuple[int, ...] = (200, 225, 250, 300, 350, 400)

#: Meteorological variables required by the correction-model features.
ERA5_VARIABLES: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "temperature",
    "specific_humidity",
    "u_component_of_wind",
    "v_component_of_wind",
    "vertical_velocity",
    "fraction_of_cloud_cover",
)

_LATITUDE_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = ("latitude", "lat")
_LONGITUDE_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = ("longitude", "lon")
_LEVEL_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = ("level", "pressure_level")
_TIME_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = ("time", "valid_time")

#: CDS NetCDF exports may use short variable names; canonical name first.
_VARIABLE_ALIASES: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {
    "temperature": ("temperature", "t"),
    "specific_humidity": ("specific_humidity", "q"),
    "u_component_of_wind": ("u_component_of_wind", "u"),
    "v_component_of_wind": ("v_component_of_wind", "v"),
    "vertical_velocity": ("vertical_velocity", "w"),
    "fraction_of_cloud_cover": ("fraction_of_cloud_cover", "cc", "tcc"),
}


def _resolve_coordinate(ds: xr.Dataset, names: tuple[str, ...], role: str) -> str:
    """Return the first coordinate name in ``names`` present in ``ds``."""
    for name in names:
        if name in ds.coords or name in ds.dims:
            return name
    raise ValueError(
        f"ERA5 dataset is missing the {role} coordinate; expected one of {list(names)}"
    )


def _to_nanosecond_utc(value: Any) -> np.datetime64:
    """Convert a datetime-like value to a tz-naive UTC ``datetime64[ns]``."""
    timestamp = pd.Timestamp(value)
    if timestamp.tzinfo is not None:
        timestamp = timestamp.tz_convert("UTC").tz_localize(None)
    return np.datetime64(timestamp, "ns")


def load_era5_dataset(file_path: str) -> xr.Dataset:
    """Load and validate an ERA5 NetCDF file.

    Validation checks the presence of the latitude, longitude, pressure
    level and time coordinates and of all :data:`ERA5_VARIABLES`. Both
    the classic ERA5 naming (``time``, ``level``, long variable names)
    and the newer CDS export naming (``valid_time``, ``pressure_level``,
    short variable names such as ``t``/``q``/``cc``) are accepted; the
    returned dataset uses canonical names. Latitude is sorted ascending
    when the file stores it descending.

    Parameters
    ----------
    file_path : str
        Path to the NetCDF file.

    Returns
    -------
    xarray.Dataset
        Validated dataset with canonical variable names, latitude
        ascending and the time coordinate renamed to ``time``.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If a required coordinate or variable is missing.
    FileNotFoundError
        If ``file_path`` does not exist.
    """
    ds = xr.open_dataset(file_path)
    latitude_name = _resolve_coordinate(ds, _LATITUDE_NAMES, "latitude")
    _resolve_coordinate(ds, _LONGITUDE_NAMES, "longitude")
    _resolve_coordinate(ds, _LEVEL_NAMES, "pressure level")
    time_name = _resolve_coordinate(ds, _TIME_NAMES, "time")

    rename_map: dict[str, str] = {}
    if time_name != "time":
        rename_map[time_name] = "time"

    missing_variables: list[str] = []
    for canonical, aliases in _VARIABLE_ALIASES.items():
        actual = next((name for name in aliases if name in ds.data_vars), None)
        if actual is None:
            missing_variables.append(canonical)
        elif actual != canonical:
            rename_map[actual] = canonical
    if missing_variables:
        raise ValueError(
            f"ERA5 dataset {file_path!r} is missing variables: {missing_variables}"
        )
    if rename_map:
        ds = ds.rename(rename_map)

    latitude = ds[latitude_name]
    if latitude.size > 1 and float(latitude[0]) > float(latitude[-1]):
        ds = ds.sortby(latitude_name)
    return ds


def interpolate_met_to_segment(
    ds_met: xr.Dataset,
    lat: float,
    lon: float,
    pressure_pa: float,
    time_dt: Any,
) -> dict[str, float]:
    """Interpolate local ambient values at a segment point.

    Performs linear multi-dimensional interpolation over latitude,
    longitude, pressure level and time. Points outside the dataset domain
    (or pressure range) return ``nan`` values, matching xarray semantics.

    Parameters
    ----------
    ds_met : xarray.Dataset
        Validated ERA5 dataset, e.g. from :func:`load_era5_dataset`.
    lat, lon : float
        Query position [deg N, deg E].
    pressure_pa : float
        Query pressure [Pa]; converted to hPa for the level axis.
    time_dt : datetime-like
        Query time; tz-aware values are converted to UTC.

    Returns
    -------
    dict[str, float]
        One entry per :data:`ERA5_VARIABLES` present in ``ds_met``.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        For non-finite coordinates, non-positive pressure, or a dataset
        missing required coordinates.
    """
    if not (math.isfinite(lat) and math.isfinite(lon)):
        raise ValueError(f"lat and lon must be finite, got {lat!r} and {lon!r}")
    if not math.isfinite(pressure_pa) or pressure_pa <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError(f"pressure_pa must be positive, got {pressure_pa!r}")

    latitude_name = _resolve_coordinate(ds_met, _LATITUDE_NAMES, "latitude")
    longitude_name = _resolve_coordinate(ds_met, _LONGITUDE_NAMES, "longitude")
    level_name = _resolve_coordinate(ds_met, _LEVEL_NAMES, "pressure level")
    time_name = _resolve_coordinate(ds_met, _TIME_NAMES, "time")

    available = [name for name in ERA5_VARIABLES if name in ds_met.data_vars]
    if not available:
        raise ValueError(
            "dataset contains none of the ERA5_VARIABLES under canonical names; "
            "run load_era5_dataset first"
        )
    subset = ds_met[available]
    interp_coordinate_names = {
        latitude_name,
        longitude_name,
        level_name,
        time_name,
    }
    # Drop auxiliary non-dimension coordinates (e.g. the string-valued
    # CDS ``expver`` track) that xarray cannot interpolate.
    auxiliary_coordinates = [
        name
        for name in subset.coords
        if name not in interp_coordinate_names and name not in subset.dims
    ]
    if auxiliary_coordinates:
        subset = subset.drop_vars(auxiliary_coordinates)
    point = subset.interp(
        {
            latitude_name: float(lat),
            longitude_name: float(lon),
            level_name: pressure_pa / 100.0,
            time_name: _to_nanosecond_utc(time_dt),
        }
    )

    values: dict[str, float] = {}
    for name in available:
        scalar = point[name].squeeze()
        if scalar.ndim > 0:
            # Collapse auxiliary dimensions (e.g. ERA5 ``expver``) by
            # taking the first index; documented schema has none.
            scalar = scalar.isel({dim: 0 for dim in scalar.dims})
        values[name] = float(scalar.values)
    return values
```

## stratum/io/meteosat.py

```python
"""Meteosat SEVIRI ingestion helpers for Project STRATUM.

Defines the SEVIRI infrared channels used by the contrail detector
(``IR_108``, ``IR_120``, ``IR_087``) and a domain cropping helper that
selects pixels inside a geographic bounding box while preserving the
native coordinate ordering (ascending or descending).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np

__all__ = ["SEVIRI_CHANNELS", "crop_to_domain"]

#: SEVIRI channels ingested for the contrail detector.
SEVIRI_CHANNELS: tuple[str, ...] = ("IR_108", "IR_120", "IR_087")


def crop_to_domain(
    image_array: np.ndarray,
    lats: np.ndarray,
    lons: np.ndarray,
    bbox: tuple[float, float, float, float],
) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:
    """Crop a geostationary image to a geographic bounding box.

    Parameters
    ----------
    image_array : numpy.ndarray
        Two-dimensional image of shape ``(len(lats), len(lons))``.
    lats : numpy.ndarray
        One-dimensional latitude coordinates [deg N] for the rows,
        ascending or descending.
    lons : numpy.ndarray
        One-dimensional longitude coordinates [deg E] for the columns,
        ascending or descending.
    bbox : tuple of float
        Bounding box as ``(north, west, south, east)`` [deg], inclusive.

    Returns
    -------
    tuple of numpy.ndarray
        ``(cropped_image, cropped_lats, cropped_lons)`` with the native
        coordinate ordering preserved.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If the bounding box is malformed, the image shape does not match
        the coordinate lengths, or the box contains no pixel centers.
    """
    if len(bbox) != 4:
        raise ValueError(f"bbox must be (north, west, south, east), got {bbox!r}")
    north, west, south, east = (float(value) for value in bbox)
    if north <= south:
        raise ValueError(f"bbox north ({north}) must exceed south ({south})")
    if east <= west:
        raise ValueError(f"bbox east ({east}) must exceed west ({west})")

    latitude_values = np.asarray(lats, dtype=float)
    longitude_values = np.asarray(lons, dtype=float)
    image = np.asarray(image_array)
    expected_shape = (latitude_values.size, longitude_values.size)
    if image.ndim != 2 or image.shape != expected_shape:
        raise ValueError(
            f"image shape {image.shape!r} does not match coordinate lengths "
            f"{expected_shape!r}"
        )

    latitude_mask = (latitude_values >= south) & (latitude_values <= north)
    longitude_mask = (longitude_values >= west) & (longitude_values <= east)
    if not latitude_mask.any() or not longitude_mask.any():
        raise ValueError(f"bounding box {bbox!r} contains no pixel centers")

    cropped = image[np.ix_(latitude_mask, longitude_mask)]
    return cropped, latitude_values[latitude_mask], longitude_values[longitude_mask]
```

## stratum/match/advect.py

```python
"""Wind advection of flight-segment coordinates on a spherical Earth."""

from __future__ import annotations

import math

__all__ = ["advect_segment", "EARTH_RADIUS_M"]

#: Spherical Earth radius used by the advection model [m].
EARTH_RADIUS_M: float = 6_371_000.0


def advect_segment(
    lat: float,
    lon: float,
    u_wind_mps: float,
    v_wind_mps: float,
    elapsed_seconds: float,
) -> tuple[float, float]:
    """Drift a coordinate forward with horizontal wind components.

    Uses a first-order local-tangent-plane approximation on a sphere:
    ``d_lat = v * t / R`` and ``d_lon = u * t / (R * cos(lat))``.

    Parameters
    ----------
    lat, lon : float
        Start coordinates [deg N, deg E].
    u_wind_mps : float
        Eastward wind component [m/s] (positive = toward east).
    v_wind_mps : float
        Northward wind component [m/s] (positive = toward north).
    elapsed_seconds : float
        Drift time [s], non-negative.

    Returns
    -------
    (float, float)
        Advected ``(lat, lon)`` [deg N, deg E]; longitude is wrapped to
        [-180, 180).

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If ``elapsed_seconds`` is negative or the start latitude is too
        close to a pole for the local approximation.
    """
    if elapsed_seconds < 0.0:
        raise ValueError(f"elapsed_seconds must be non-negative, got {elapsed_seconds}")

    cos_lat = math.cos(math.radians(lat))
    if abs(cos_lat) < 1e-12:
        raise ValueError(f"latitude {lat} is too close to a pole for advection")

    new_lat = lat + math.degrees(v_wind_mps * elapsed_seconds / EARTH_RADIUS_M)
    new_lon = lon + math.degrees(
        u_wind_mps * elapsed_seconds / (EARTH_RADIUS_M * cos_lat)
    )
    wrapped_lon = (new_lon + 180.0) % 360.0 - 180.0
    return new_lat, wrapped_lon
```

## stratum/match/label.py

```python
"""Flight-contrail association and tri-state labeling.

A flight segment (after wind advection and parallax correction) is
matched against satellite detections by centroid distance and line
orientation. The outcome is encoded as a tri-state label:

- ``1.0``  positive: a detection is associated with the segment,
- ``0.0``  observable non-detection: the scene was observed in at least
  two satellite slots and no line was found,
- ``nan``  unknown/unobservable: too few observable slots, so no
  statement can be made. NaN is never coerced to 0.0.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from typing import Any, Mapping, Sequence

__all__ = [
    "match_segment_to_detections",
    "assign_tristate_label",
    "haversine_km",
    "EARTH_RADIUS_M",
]

#: Spherical Earth radius used for great-circle distances [m].
EARTH_RADIUS_M: float = 6_371_000.0


def haversine_km(
    coord1: tuple[float, float], coord2: tuple[float, float]
) -> float:
    """Great-circle distance between two (lat, lon) coordinates [km]."""
    lat1, lon1 = math.radians(coord1[0]), math.radians(coord1[1])
    lat2, lon2 = math.radians(coord2[0]), math.radians(coord2[1])
    dlat = lat2 - lat1
    dlon = lon2 - lon1
    a = (
        math.sin(dlat / 2.0) ** 2
        + math.cos(lat1) * math.cos(lat2) * math.sin(dlon / 2.0) ** 2
    )
    return 2.0 * EARTH_RADIUS_M * math.asin(min(1.0, math.sqrt(a))) / 1000.0


def _angle_difference_deg(angle_a: float, angle_b: float) -> float:
    """Smallest orientation difference in [0, 90] degrees (mod 180)."""
    difference = abs(angle_a - angle_b) % 180.0
    return min(difference, 180.0 - difference)


def match_segment_to_detections(
    advected_parallax_coord: tuple[float, float],
    segment_heading_deg: float,
    detected_objects: Sequence[Mapping[str, Any]],
    distance_tolerance_km: float = 10.0,
    angle_tolerance_deg: float = 20.0,
) -> bool:
    """Match an advected, parallax-corrected segment to detections.

    A match requires at least one detected object whose centroid lies
    within ``distance_tolerance_km`` of the segment coordinate and whose
    orientation agrees with ``segment_heading_deg`` within
    ``angle_tolerance_deg`` (mod 180 degrees).

    Parameters
    ----------
    advected_parallax_coord : (float, float)
        Segment coordinate after wind advection and parallax correction,
        as ``(lat, lon)`` [deg].
    segment_heading_deg : float
        Segment flight heading [deg] (any 360-degree convention).
    detected_objects : sequence of mapping
        Detections, each with keys ``centroid`` (``(lat, lon)`` [deg])
        and ``angle_deg`` (line orientation in any 180-degree
        convention).
    distance_tolerance_km : float, optional
        Maximum centroid distance for a match [km], by default 10.0.
    angle_tolerance_deg : float, optional
        Maximum orientation difference for a match [deg], by default 20.0.

    Returns
    -------
    bool
        True if any detection satisfies both tolerances.
    """
    for obj in detected_objects:
        centroid = obj["centroid"]
        object_lat = float(centroid[0])
        object_lon = float(centroid[1])
        if (
            haversine_km(advected_parallax_coord, (object_lat, object_lon))
            > distance_tolerance_km
        ):
            continue
        object_angle = float(obj["angle_deg"])
        if (
            _angle_difference_deg(object_angle, segment_heading_deg)
            <= angle_tolerance_deg
        ):
            return True
    return False


def assign_tristate_label(matched: bool, observable_slots: int) -> float:
    """Assign the tri-state flight-contrail label.

    Parameters
    ----------
    matched : bool
        True when a satellite detection was associated with the segment.
    observable_slots : int
        Number of satellite slots in which the scene passed the
        observability gate.

    Returns
    -------
    float
        ``1.0`` when matched; ``0.0`` when not matched but the scene was
        observable in at least two slots (a genuine observable
        non-detection); otherwise ``nan`` (unknown/unobservable).
        NaN is never coerced to 0.0.
    """
    if matched:
        return 1.0
    if observable_slots >= 2:
        return 0.0
    return float("nan")
```

## stratum/match/observe.py

```python
"""Scene observability gate for satellite contrail screening."""

from __future__ import annotations

__all__ = ["is_scene_observable"]

#: Maximum high-cloud fraction allowed for a usable scene.
MAX_HIGH_CLOUD_FRACTION: float = 0.5
#: Maximum viewing zenith angle allowed for a usable scene [deg].
MAX_VIEWING_ZENITH_ANGLE_DEG: float = 60.0


def is_scene_observable(
    high_cloud_fraction: float,
    viewing_zenith_angle_deg: float,
    opaque_cloud: bool = False,
) -> bool:
    """Return True only when a satellite scene is usable for screening.

    A scene is observable if and only if all three conditions hold:

    - ``high_cloud_fraction < 0.5`` (contrails are not masked by high
      cloud in the field of view),
    - ``viewing_zenith_angle_deg < 60.0`` (avoid strong geometric
      distortion and parallax at the swath edge),
    - ``opaque_cloud`` is False (no fully opaque cloud screens the scene).

    Parameters
    ----------
    high_cloud_fraction : float
        Fraction of the scene covered by high cloud [0-1].
    viewing_zenith_angle_deg : float
        Satellite viewing zenith angle [deg].
    opaque_cloud : bool, optional
        True when the scene contains opaque cloud, by default False.

    Returns
    -------
    bool
        True if the scene is observable, False otherwise.
    """
    return (
        high_cloud_fraction < MAX_HIGH_CLOUD_FRACTION
        and viewing_zenith_angle_deg < MAX_VIEWING_ZENITH_ANGLE_DEG
        and not opaque_cloud
    )
```

## stratum/match/parallax.py

```python
"""Meteosat-9 parallax displacement for elevated targets.

A geostationary satellite images a target at altitude along a slanted
line of sight. The satellite navigation usually geolocates the pixel at
the Earth ellipsoid surface, so the apparent ground position is
displaced away from the sub-satellite point relative to the true
sub-target position. The displacement is roughly ``alt_m * tan(zeta)``,
where ``zeta`` is the satellite zenith angle at the target.

The ray from the satellite through the target is intersected with a
spherical Earth; the near-side entry point (just beyond the target) is
the apparent surface location. Coordinates are geocentric on a sphere.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math

__all__ = [
    "calculate_parallax_displacement",
    "EARTH_RADIUS_M",
    "METEOSAT9_ALTITUDE_M",
    "METEOSAT9_LONGITUDE_DEG",
]

#: Spherical Earth radius used by the parallax model [m].
EARTH_RADIUS_M: float = 6_371_000.0
#: Meteosat-9 geostationary altitude above the surface [m].
METEOSAT9_ALTITUDE_M: float = 35_786_000.0
#: Meteosat-9 nominal sub-satellite longitude [deg E].
METEOSAT9_LONGITUDE_DEG: float = 45.5
#: Meteosat-9 sub-satellite latitude [deg N].
METEOSAT9_LATITUDE_DEG: float = 0.0


def _to_cartesian(
    lat_deg: float, lon_deg: float, radius_m: float
) -> tuple[float, float, float]:
    """Convert geocentric (lat, lon, radius) to Cartesian ECEF [m]."""
    lat_rad, lon_rad = math.radians(lat_deg), math.radians(lon_deg)
    cos_lat = math.cos(lat_rad)
    return (
        radius_m * cos_lat * math.cos(lon_rad),
        radius_m * cos_lat * math.sin(lon_rad),
        radius_m * math.sin(lat_rad),
    )


def _to_geodetic(x: float, y: float, z: float) -> tuple[float, float]:
    """Convert Cartesian ECEF [m] to geocentric (lat, lon) [deg]."""
    radius = math.sqrt(x * x + y * y + z * z)
    lat_deg = math.degrees(math.asin(z / radius))
    lon_deg = math.degrees(math.atan2(y, x))
    return lat_deg, lon_deg


def calculate_parallax_displacement(
    lat: float, lon: float, alt_m: float
) -> tuple[float, float]:
    """Apparent Meteosat-9 displacement of an elevated target.

    Parameters
    ----------
    lat, lon : float
        True target coordinates [deg N, deg E].
    alt_m : float
        Target altitude above the spherical Earth [m].

    Returns
    -------
    (float, float)
        Apparent minus true displacement ``(delta_lat_deg, delta_lon_deg)``,
        pointing away from the sub-satellite point (0 deg N, 45.5 deg E).
        Add it to the true coordinate to obtain the location that the
        satellite image reports.
    """
    satellite = _to_cartesian(
        METEOSAT9_LATITUDE_DEG,
        METEOSAT9_LONGITUDE_DEG,
        EARTH_RADIUS_M + METEOSAT9_ALTITUDE_M,
    )
    target = _to_cartesian(lat, lon, EARTH_RADIUS_M + alt_m)
    direction = tuple(t - s for t, s in zip(target, satellite))

    # Solve |satellite + t * direction|^2 = EARTH_RADIUS_M^2 for the
    # surface intersection beyond the target.
    a = sum(component * component for component in direction)
    b = 2.0 * sum(s * d for s, d in zip(satellite, direction))
    c = sum(s * s for s in satellite) - EARTH_RADIUS_M**2
    discriminant = math.sqrt(b * b - 4.0 * a * c)
    # The near-side entry root (minus sign) is the first surface crossing
    # beyond the target; the plus root is the exit point on the far side
    # of the planet.
    t = (-b - discriminant) / (2.0 * a)
    surface = tuple(s + t * d for s, d in zip(satellite, direction))

    apparent_lat, apparent_lon = _to_geodetic(*surface)
    return apparent_lat - lat, apparent_lon - lon
```

## stratum/model/calibrate.py

```python
"""Isotonic probability calibration for the STRATUM correction model.

Wraps a fitted binary classifier together with an isotonic regression map
fitted on validation predictions, so the calibrated output is a true
probability in ``[0.0, 1.0]``. Isotonic regression is monotonically
non-decreasing, therefore the physical monotone constraints of the base
model are preserved by calibration.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol

import numpy as np
from sklearn.isotonic import IsotonicRegression

__all__ = ["CalibratedModel", "fit_calibrator"]


class PredictProbaModel(Protocol):
    """Protocol for estimators exposing sklearn-style ``predict_proba``."""

    def predict_proba(self, X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        """Return class probabilities of shape ``(n_samples, 2)``."""
        ...


class CalibratedModel:
    """Isotonic-calibrated wrapper around a binary classifier.

    Parameters
    ----------
    model : PredictProbaModel
        Fitted base classifier.
    calibrator : IsotonicRegression
        Isotonic map fitted on the base model's positive-class
        probabilities.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        model: PredictProbaModel,
        calibrator: IsotonicRegression,
    ) -> None:
        self._model = model
        self._calibrator = calibrator

    @property
    def model(self) -> PredictProbaModel:
        """The wrapped base classifier."""
        return self._model

    @property
    def calibrator(self) -> IsotonicRegression:
        """The fitted isotonic calibration map."""
        return self._calibrator

    def predict_raw_proba(self, X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        """Positive-class probability of the base model, shape ``(n,)``."""
        features = np.atleast_2d(np.asarray(X, dtype=float))
        raw = np.asarray(self._model.predict_proba(features), dtype=float)
        return raw[:, 1]

    def predict_proba(self, X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        """Calibrated class probabilities of shape ``(n_samples, 2)``.

        Column 1 holds the calibrated probability of persistence.
        Predictions outside the fitted range are clipped, so the output
        always lies within ``[0.0, 1.0]``.
        """
        positive = self._calibrator.predict(self.predict_raw_proba(X))
        positive = np.clip(positive, 0.0, 1.0)
        return np.column_stack([1.0 - positive, positive])

    def predict(self, X: np.ndarray, threshold: float = 0.5) -> np.ndarray:
        """Binary decisions from the calibrated positive probability."""
        return (self.predict_proba(X)[:, 1] >= threshold).astype(int)


def fit_calibrator(
    model: PredictProbaModel,
    X_val: np.ndarray,
    y_val: np.ndarray,
) -> CalibratedModel:
    """Fit isotonic calibration on non-NaN validation rows.

    Parameters
    ----------
    model : PredictProbaModel
        Fitted base classifier.
    X_val : np.ndarray
        Validation features, shape ``(n_samples, n_features)``.
    y_val : np.ndarray
        Validation labels; NaN rows (unobservable scenes) are excluded
        from the isotonic fit.

    Returns
    -------
    CalibratedModel
        Wrapper whose ``predict_proba`` returns calibrated probabilities
        clipped to ``[0.0, 1.0]``.
    """
    features = np.asarray(X_val, dtype=float)
    labels = np.asarray(y_val, dtype=float)
    if features.ndim != 2 or labels.shape != (features.shape[0],):
        raise ValueError(
            "X_val and y_val shapes do not line up: "
            f"{features.shape!r} and {labels.shape!r}"
        )
    valid = ~np.isnan(labels)
    if not valid.any():
        raise ValueError("all validation labels are NaN; nothing to calibrate on")

    raw = np.asarray(model.predict_proba(features[valid]), dtype=float)[:, 1]
    calibrator = IsotonicRegression(
        increasing=True,
        out_of_bounds="clip",
        y_min=0.0,
        y_max=1.0,
    )
    calibrator.fit(raw, labels[valid])
    return CalibratedModel(model, calibrator)
```

## stratum/model/features.py

```python
"""Frozen feature schema and row assembly for the STRATUM correction model.

The canonical order in ``FEATURE_NAMES`` is the model contract: engine
microphysics (propulsion efficiency, soot number emission index),
atmospheric turbulence (gradient Richardson number), and radiative
geometry (solar zenith angle) extend the previous physics/time block. The
order must not change without retraining. Helper :func:`encode_cyclical`
remains available for callers that build their own encodings.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from typing import Mapping

import numpy as np

__all__ = [
    "FEATURE_NAMES",
    "encode_cyclical",
    "extract_features_row",
    "feature_vector",
]

#: Canonical feature order for the correction model (Section 12.2).
FEATURE_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = (
    # Relative humidity over ice: point value and vertical structure.
    "rhi",
    "rhi_above",
    "rhi_below",
    "rhi_grad",
    # Temperature and pressure state.
    "temp_k",
    "tlm_k",
    "tlc_k",
    "pressure_pa",
    # Cloud and dynamics.
    "cloud_ice",
    "high_cloud_fraction",
    "vertical_velocity_omega",
    "vertical_wind_shear",
    # Vertical distance to the tropopause and horizontal position.
    "dist_to_tropopause_m",
    "lat",
    "lon",
    # Time.
    "month",
    "local_solar_time_hours",
    # Atmospheric diagnostics.
    "richardson_number",
    "solar_zenith_deg",
    # Engine microphysics.
    "engine_efficiency_eta",
    "log_soot_ei_n",
)

#: Neutral defaults for the diagnostic/engine features.
DEFAULT_RICHARDSON_NUMBER: float = 1.0
DEFAULT_SOLAR_ZENITH_DEG: float = 100.0
DEFAULT_ENGINE_EFFICIENCY_ETA: float = 0.32
DEFAULT_LOG_SOOT_EI_N: float = 15.0


def encode_cyclical(val: float, period: float) -> tuple[float, float]:
    """Encode a cyclical value as a unit-circle ``(sin, cos)`` pair.

    ``val`` is mapped onto the angle ``2 * pi * val / period`` so that
    values one full period apart coincide and the encoding is continuous
    across the wrap-around (e.g. month 12 and month 1).
    """
    if period <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError(f"period must be positive, got {period!r}")
    angle = 2.0 * math.pi * val / period
    return math.sin(angle), math.cos(angle)


def extract_features_row(
    *,
    rhi: float,
    rhi_above: float,
    rhi_below: float,
    rhi_grad: float,
    temp_k: float,
    tlm_k: float,
    tlc_k: float,
    pressure_pa: float,
    cloud_ice: float,
    high_cloud_fraction: float,
    vertical_velocity_omega: float,
    vertical_wind_shear: float,
    dist_to_tropopause_m: float,
    lat: float,
    lon: float,
    month: float,
    local_solar_time_hours: float,
    richardson_number: float = DEFAULT_RICHARDSON_NUMBER,
    solar_zenith_deg: float = DEFAULT_SOLAR_ZENITH_DEG,
    engine_efficiency_eta: float = DEFAULT_ENGINE_EFFICIENCY_ETA,
    log_soot_ei_n: float = DEFAULT_LOG_SOOT_EI_N,
) -> dict[str, float]:
    """Assemble one sample as a feature dictionary in canonical order.

    ``tlm_k`` and ``tlc_k`` are the Schmidt-Appleman threshold
    temperatures [K]. ``month`` (1-12) and ``local_solar_time_hours``
    (0-24) enter as raw values; ``richardson_number`` (gradient Ri),
    ``solar_zenith_deg`` (0-180), ``engine_efficiency_eta`` (0-1) and
    ``log_soot_ei_n`` (log10 of the nvPM number emission index) default to
    the neutral values used when a diagnostic is unavailable.

    Returns
    -------
    dict[str, float]
        Mapping keyed by :data:`FEATURE_NAMES`, in canonical order.
    """
    return {
        "rhi": float(rhi),
        "rhi_above": float(rhi_above),
        "rhi_below": float(rhi_below),
        "rhi_grad": float(rhi_grad),
        "temp_k": float(temp_k),
        "tlm_k": float(tlm_k),
        "tlc_k": float(tlc_k),
        "pressure_pa": float(pressure_pa),
        "cloud_ice": float(cloud_ice),
        "high_cloud_fraction": float(high_cloud_fraction),
        "vertical_velocity_omega": float(vertical_velocity_omega),
        "vertical_wind_shear": float(vertical_wind_shear),
        "dist_to_tropopause_m": float(dist_to_tropopause_m),
        "lat": float(lat),
        "lon": float(lon),
        "month": float(month),
        "local_solar_time_hours": float(local_solar_time_hours),
        "richardson_number": float(richardson_number),
        "solar_zenith_deg": float(solar_zenith_deg),
        "engine_efficiency_eta": float(engine_efficiency_eta),
        "log_soot_ei_n": float(log_soot_ei_n),
    }


def feature_vector(row: Mapping[str, float]) -> np.ndarray:
    """Convert a feature dictionary into a vector in canonical order.

    Raises
    ------
    KeyError
        If ``row`` misses any feature from :data:`FEATURE_NAMES`.
    """
    missing = [name for name in FEATURE_NAMES if name not in row]
    if missing:
        raise KeyError(f"row is missing features: {missing}")
    return np.array([float(row[name]) for name in FEATURE_NAMES], dtype=float)
```

## stratum/model/train.py

```python
"""Monotone LightGBM training for the STRATUM correction model.

The classifier is constrained to stay physically consistent: ice
supersaturation (``rhi``) and warmer ``tlm_k`` thresholds move the
persistence risk one way or the other per :data:`MONOTONE_CONSTRAINTS`,
while lower Richardson numbers (turbulent shear), higher engine
efficiency, and higher soot number emission indices enter the engine and
turbulence microphysics. Rows whose label is NaN (unobservable scenes,
e.g. opaque cloud) are strictly masked out and never treated as
negatives.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Sequence

import lightgbm as lgb
import numpy as np

__all__ = [
    "MONOTONE_CONSTRAINTS",
    "monotone_constraints_for",
    "train_contrail_model",
    "compute_brier_score",
]

#: Native LightGBM monotone constraint per feature: +1 increasing, -1 decreasing.
MONOTONE_CONSTRAINTS: dict[str, int] = {
    "rhi": 1,
    "tlm_k": -1,
    "richardson_number": -1,
    "engine_efficiency_eta": 1,
    "log_soot_ei_n": 1,
}

#: Fixed LightGBM hyperparameters; ``random_state`` fixes reproducibility.
_MODEL_PARAMS: dict[str, object] = {
    "objective": "binary",
    "n_estimators": 300,
    "learning_rate": 0.05,
    "num_leaves": 31,
    "min_child_samples": 20,
    "monotone_constraints_method": "intermediate",
    "random_state": 42,
    "verbosity": -1,
}


def monotone_constraints_for(feature_names: Sequence[str]) -> list[int]:
    """Order LightGBM monotone constraints to match ``feature_names``.

    Features absent from :data:`MONOTONE_CONSTRAINTS` are unconstrained
    (entry ``0``).
    """
    return [MONOTONE_CONSTRAINTS.get(name, 0) for name in feature_names]


def train_contrail_model(
    X: np.ndarray,
    y: np.ndarray,
    feature_names: list[str],
) -> lgb.LGBMClassifier:
    """Fit a monotone binary LightGBM classifier on labelled segments.

    Parameters
    ----------
    X : np.ndarray
        Feature matrix of shape ``(n_samples, n_features)`` in the
        canonical order of ``feature_names``. NaN feature values are
        supported natively by LightGBM.
    y : np.ndarray
        Tri-state labels: ``1.0`` (persistent), ``0.0`` (not persistent)
        or ``NaN`` (unobservable). Rows with NaN labels are masked out;
        they are never coerced to ``0.0``.
    feature_names : list[str]
        Feature names in the same column order as ``X``.

    Returns
    -------
    lgb.LGBMClassifier
        Fitted classifier using binary logloss, ``random_state=42``, and
        native monotone constraints from :data:`MONOTONE_CONSTRAINTS`.
    """
    features = np.asarray(X, dtype=float)
    labels = np.asarray(y, dtype=float)
    if features.ndim != 2:
        raise ValueError(f"X must be 2-D, got shape {features.shape!r}")
    if labels.ndim != 1 or labels.shape[0] != features.shape[0]:
        raise ValueError(
            "y must be 1-D with one label per row of X, got shapes "
            f"{labels.shape!r} and {features.shape!r}"
        )
    if len(feature_names) != features.shape[1]:
        raise ValueError(
            f"feature_names has {len(feature_names)} entries but X has "
            f"{features.shape[1]} columns"
        )

    valid = ~np.isnan(labels)
    if not valid.any():
        raise ValueError("all labels are NaN; nothing to train on")
    labels_valid = labels[valid]
    if not np.all(np.isin(labels_valid, (0.0, 1.0))):
        raise ValueError("labels must be 0.0, 1.0, or NaN")

    model = lgb.LGBMClassifier(
        monotone_constraints=monotone_constraints_for(feature_names),
        **_MODEL_PARAMS,
    )
    model.fit(features[valid], labels_valid)
    return model


def compute_brier_score(y_true: np.ndarray, y_prob: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """Mean squared error between outcomes and predicted probabilities.

    Non-finite entries of either array (e.g. masked NaN labels) are
    excluded from the mean. This matches
    ``sklearn.metrics.brier_score_loss`` on clean binary inputs.
    """
    outcomes = np.asarray(y_true, dtype=float)
    probabilities = np.asarray(y_prob, dtype=float)
    if outcomes.shape != probabilities.shape:
        raise ValueError(
            "y_true and y_prob must have the same shape, got "
            f"{outcomes.shape!r} and {probabilities.shape!r}"
        )
    valid = np.isfinite(outcomes) & np.isfinite(probabilities)
    if not valid.any():
        raise ValueError("no finite (label, probability) pairs to score")
    return float(np.mean((probabilities[valid] - outcomes[valid]) ** 2))
```
